Simpler and honest: don't release; document that savepoint is released by the enclosing commit/rollback. Most DBs free savepoints at commit. Each account gets a fresh random salt and a PBKDF2 derived key\n * (salt and key stored as SQL binary columns, algorithm and iteration count stored alongside so\n * the work factor can be raised later without invalidating old rows). Verification re-derives the\n * key from the supplied password and the stored salt and compares the two in constant time.\n *\n * <p>Instances are immutable and safe for concurrent use; {@link Connection} itself is not, so a\n * connection should not be shared between threads without external synchronisation.\n */\npublic final class UserAccountService {\n\n    /** Key-derivation function recommended for new deployments. */\n    public static final String DEFAULT_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n\n    /**\n     * PBKDF2 rounds for new accounts. Chosen to match the OWASP 2023 guidance for\n     * PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 (310 000 iterations); raise it if your hardware is faster than the\n     * reference measurement reported by {@link #deriveMillis()}.\n     */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 310_000;\n\n    /** Bytes of fresh randomness per account. 128 bits is far beyond any collision concern. */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_SALT_BYTES = 16;\n\n    /** Bytes of derived key stored per account (256 bits, the full PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 output). */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_HASH_BYTES = 32;\n\n    /** Rejected above this: a corrupted row must not be able to make us spin for minutes. */\n    private static final int MAX_STORED_ITERATIONS = 20_000_000;\n\n    /** Rejected below this: a corrupted row must not quietly downgrade every account to no work. */\n    private static final int MIN_STORED_ITERATIONS = 1_000;\n\n    private static final String TABLE = \"app_user\";\n\n    private static final String CREATE_TABLE =\n            \"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \" + TABLE + \" (\"\n            + \" username VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,\"\n            + \" algorithm VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,\"\n            + \" iterations INT NOT NULL,\"\n            + \" salt VARBINARY(128) NOT NULL,\"\n            + \" password_hash VARBINARY(128) NOT NULL,\"\n            + \" created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\"\n            + \" CONSTRAINT pk_\" + TABLE + \" PRIMARY KEY (username)\"\n            + \")\";\n\n    private static final String INSERT_USER =\n            \"INSERT INTO \" + TABLE\n            + \" (username, algorithm, iterations, salt, password_hash) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\";\n\n    // The predicate is an exact equality on a bound parameter: no LIKE, no case folding, no\n    // concatenation, so neither SQL injection nor a widened match is possible.\n    private static final String SELECT_USER =\n            \"SELECT algorithm, iterations, salt, password_hash FROM \" + TABLE\n            + \" WHERE username = ?\";\n\n    private static final String DELETE_USER = \"DELETE FROM \" + TABLE + \" WHERE username = ?\";\n\n    private static final String DROP_TABLE = \"DROP TABLE IF EXISTS \" + TABLE;\n\n    private static final Pattern USERNAME_PATTERN = Pattern.compile(\"[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{2,63}\");\n\n    private static final int MIN_PASSWORD_CHARS = 10;\n    private static final int MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS = 1024;\n\n    private static final SecureRandom RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n    private static final AtomicLong SAVEPOINT_SEQ = new AtomicLong();\n\n    private final String algorithm;\n    private final int iterations;\n    private final int saltBytes;\n    private final int hashBytes;\n\n    /**\n     * Salt and derived key for a password that no account can ever have. Used to spend the same\n     * key-derivation time on an unknown username as on a known one, so the response time does not\n     * disclose which accounts exist. Built lazily by running the real KDF, never hand-written.\n     */\n    private volatile byte[] dummySalt;\n    private volatile byte[] dummyKey;\n    private char[] dummyPassword;\n\n    /** An instance using the recommended parameters. */\n    public UserAccountService() {\n        this(DEFAULT_ALGORITHM, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, DEFAULT_SALT_BYTES, DEFAULT_HASH_BYTES);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * An instance with an explicit work factor, for deployments that need to tune the\n     * cost/latency trade-off or for tests that cannot afford 300 000 rounds per call.\n     *\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the parameters are unusable\n     */\n    public UserAccountService(String algorithm, int iterations, int saltBytes, int hashBytes) {\n        if (algorithm == null || !algorithm.startsWith(\"PBKDF2\")) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"algorithm must be a PBKDF2 variant, got: \" + algorithm);\n        }\n        if (iterations < MIN_STORED_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_STORED_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                    \"iterations must be in [\" + MIN_STORED_ITERATIONS + \", \" + MAX_STORED_ITERATIONS + \"]\");\n        }\n        if (saltBytes < 8 || saltBytes > 64) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"saltBytes must be in [8, 64]\");\n        }\n        if (hashBytes < 16 || hashBytes > 64) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"hashBytes must be in [16, 64]\");\n        }\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(algorithm);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"algorithm not available on this JVM: \" + algorithm, e);\n        }\n        this.algorithm = algorithm;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.saltBytes = saltBytes;\n        this.hashBytes = hashBytes;\n    }\n\n    /** The recommended number of PBKDF2 rounds for accounts created by this instance. */\n    public int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Creates the account table if it is not already there. The DDL uses only portable types\n     * ({@code VARBINARY} is {@code BYTEA} on PostgreSQL if you need to hand-edit it) so the same\n     * call works on H2, PostgreSQL, MySQL/MariaDB, SQL Server, Oracle and SQLite.\n     *\n     * <p>Primary-key-on-username is what makes registration race-safe: two concurrent inserts of\n     * the same username cannot both succeed, and the loser gets\n     * {@link DuplicateUsernameException} rather than a silently duplicated row.\n     */\n    public void ensureSchema(Connection connection) throws SQLException {\n        try (Statement st = connection.createStatement()) {\n            st.execute(CREATE_TABLE);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Removes the account table. Intended for tests and demos. */\n    public void dropSchema(Connection connection) throws SQLException {\n        try (Statement st = connection.createStatement()) {\n            st.execute(DROP_TABLE);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Creates an account for {@code username} with {@code password}.\n     *\n     * <p>The caller's transaction (if any) stays usable when the username is already taken: the\n     * insert runs inside a savepoint that is rolled back on failure, which matters on engines such\n     * as PostgreSQL where a failed statement otherwise aborts the whole transaction.\n     *\n     * @param password validated but not cleared; wipe the caller's array if it matters to you\n     * @throws InvalidValueException the username or password breaks the documented policy\n     * @throws DuplicateUsernameException {@code username} is already registered\n     * @throws SQLException from the database\n     */\n    public Account registerUser(Connection connection, String username, char[] password)\n            throws SQLException, DuplicateUsernameException {\n        validateUsername(username);\n        validatePassword(password);\n\n        byte[] salt = new byte[saltBytes];\n        RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n        byte[] hash = derive(password, salt, iterations, algorithm, hashBytes);\n\n        // A single INSERT is the whole write, so there is no partially registered account to clean\n        // up, and \"check then insert\" (which would let two racing requests both pass the check) is\n        // replaced by \"insert and read back the unique-constraint violation\".\n        Savepoint savepoint = null;\n        if (!connection.getAutoCommit()) {\n            savepoint = connection.setSavepoint(\"REG_\" + SAVEPOINT_SEQ.incrementAndGet());\n        }\n        try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(INSERT_USER)) {\n            insert.setString(1, username);\n            insert.setString(2, algorithm);\n            insert.setInt(3, iterations);\n            insert.setBytes(4, salt);\n            insert.setBytes(5, hash);\n            insert.executeUpdate();\n        } catch (SQLException e) {\n            if (savepoint != null) {\n                connection.rollback(savepoint);\n            }\n            if (isUniqueConstraintViolation(e)) {\n                throw new DuplicateUsernameException(username);\n            }\n            throw e;\n        }\n        return new Account(username, algorithm, iterations);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Convenience overload for callers that already hold a {@link String}. A {@code String} cannot\n     * be wiped from memory afterwards, so prefer the {@code char[]} form when the password came\n     * from a console or a request body you control.\n     */\n    public Account registerUser(Connection connection, String username, String password)\n            throws SQLException, DuplicateUsernameException {\n        if (password == null) {\n            throw new InvalidValueException(\"password must not be null\");\n        }\n        return registerUser(connection, username, password.toCharArray());\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Checks {@code username} / {@code password} against the stored credential.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} only if the account exists and the password re-derives to the stored key\n     * @throws SQLException from the database\n     */\n    public boolean authenticate(Connection connection, String username, char[] password)\n            throws SQLException {\n        // Reject malformed input before touching the database, but do not reveal which half was bad\n        // and do not let an empty username skip the constant-work path below.\n        if (username == null || password == null || username.isEmpty() || password.length == 0) {\n            burnKeyDerivation();\n            return false;\n        }\n        StoredCredential stored;\n        try (PreparedStatement select = connection.prepareStatement(SELECT_USER)) {\n            select.setString(1, username);\n            try (ResultSet rs = select.executeQuery()) {\n                if (!rs.next()) {\n                    // Same cost as a real verification: an attacker timing logins cannot tell an\n                    // existing account from a missing one.\n                    burnKeyDerivation();\n                    return false;\n                }\n                stored = readCredential(rs, username);\n            }\n        }\n        byte[] candidate = derive(password, stored.salt, stored.iterations, stored.algorithm,\n                stored.hash.length);\n        // Length-independent constant-time comparison over fixed-size keys.\n        return MessageDigest.isEqual(stored.hash, candidate);\n    }\n\n    /** Convenience {@link String} overload of {@link #authenticate(Connection, String, char[])}. */\n    public boolean authenticate(Connection connection, String username, String password)\n            throws SQLException {\n        return password != null && authenticate(connection, username, password.toCharArray());\n    }\n\n    /** Deletes an account. Returns {@code true} if a row was removed. */\n    public boolean deleteUser(Connection connection, String username) throws SQLException {\n        try (PreparedStatement delete = connection.prepareStatement(DELETE_USER)) {\n            delete.setString(1, username);\n            return delete.executeUpdate() == 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether stored credentials created by an older, cheaper work factor should be re-derived at\n     * the current {@link #iterations()}. Call with the iteration count read back from a row; the\n     * usual place to act on {@code true} is a successful login, when the plaintext password is\n     * available anyway.\n     */\n    public boolean credentialsNeedUpgrade(int storedIterations) {\n        return storedIterations < iterations;\n    }\n\n    /** Milliseconds for one key derivation with this instance's parameters; for tuning. */\n    public long deriveMillis() {\n        char[] probe = \"tuning-only-passphrase-0123456789\".toCharArray();\n        byte[] salt = new byte[saltBytes];\n        long start = System.nanoTime();\n        derive(probe, salt, iterations, algorithm, hashBytes);\n        return (System.nanoTime() - start) / 1_000_000L;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates the bits read back from a row before spending any work on them: a tampered or\n     * truncated row must be refused, not fed to {@link PBEKeySpec} (which happily accepts an empty\n     * salt) and not allowed to select an unbounded iteration count.\n     */\n    private StoredCredential readCredential(ResultSet rs, String username) throws SQLException {\n        String storedAlgorithm = rs.getString(1);\n        int storedIterations = rs.getInt(2);\n        byte[] salt = rs.getBytes(3);\n        byte[] hash = rs.getBytes(4);\n        if (storedAlgorithm == null || !storedAlgorithm.startsWith(\"PBKDF2\")) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (storedIterations < MIN_STORED_ITERATIONS || storedIterations > MAX_STORED_ITERATIONS) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (salt == null || salt.length < 8 || salt.length > 128) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (hash == null || hash.length < 16 || hash.length > 128) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return new StoredCredential(storedAlgorithm, storedIterations, salt, hash);\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isUniqueConstraintViolation(SQLException e) {\n        if (e instanceof SQLIntegrityConstraintViolationException) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        // Drivers that do not use the JDBC 4 subclass still report the integrity-constraint class\n        // 23 in SQLState (23000 on MySQL/SQL Server, 23505 on PostgreSQL/H2, 23000 on Oracle).\n        String sqlState = e.getSQLState();\n        return sqlState != null && sqlState.startsWith(\"23\");\n    }\n\n    private static void validateUsername(String username) {\n        if (username == null || !USERNAME_PATTERN.matcher(username).matches()) {\n            throw new InvalidValueException(\n                    \"username must be 3-64 characters of [A-Za-z0-9._-] and start with a letter or digit\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void validatePassword(char[] password) {\n        if (password == null) {\n            throw new InvalidValueException(\"password must not be null\");\n        }\n        if (password.length < MIN_PASSWORD_CHARS || password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS) {\n            throw new InvalidValueException(\"password must be \" + MIN_PASSWORD_CHARS + \"-\"\n                    + MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS + \" characters long\");\n        }\n        for (char c : password) {\n            if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7F) {\n                throw new InvalidValueException(\"password must not contain control characters\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int iterations, String algorithm,\n            int hashBytes) {\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, iterations, hashBytes * 8);\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(algorithm);\n            return factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            // Only reachable if the row names a KDF this JVM does not ship, which readCredential\n            // already screens for; a wrong answer here must not look like \"wrong password\".\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"cannot derive key with \" + algorithm, e);\n        } finally {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Runs one key derivation that can never authenticate anything, so that a lookup that found no\n     * row costs the same as one that did.\n     */\n    private void burnKeyDerivation() {\n        byte[] salt = dummySalt;\n        byte[] key = dummyKey;\n        char[] password = dummyPassword;\n        if (salt == null || key == null || password == null) {\n            synchronized (this) {\n                if (dummySalt == null) {\n                    char[] probe = randomPassword();\n                    byte[] probeSalt = new byte[saltBytes];\n                    RANDOM.nextBytes(probeSalt);\n                    // Real KDF over a password that exists in no table, so the dummy digest is\n                    // structurally identical to a stored one instead of a hand-written constant\n                    // that a decoder might reject and silently skip the work.\n                    byte[] probeKey = derive(probe, probeSalt, iterations, algorithm, hashBytes);\n                    dummyPassword = probe;\n                    dummySalt = probeSalt;\n                    dummyKey = probeKey;\n                }\n                salt = dummySalt;\n                key = dummyKey;\n                password = private static void testDelete(UserAccountService svc, Connection c) throws Exception {\n        svc.registerUser(c, \"doomed\", GOOD);\n        ok(\"delete removes the account\", svc.deleteUser(c, private static void testConcurrentDuplicate(UserAccountService svc, Connection shared)\n            throws Exception {\n        final CountDownLatch go = new CountDownLatch(1);\n        ExecutorService pool = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(2);\n        Callable<String> racer = () -> {\n            go.await();\n            try {\n                svc.registerUser(shared, \"racer\", GOOD);\n                return \"registered\";\n            } catch (UserAccountService.DuplicateUsernameException e) {\n                return \"duplicate\";\n            }\n        };\n        Future<String> f1 = pool.submit(racer);\n        Future<String> f2 = pool.submit(racer);\n        go.countDown();\n        List<String> results = Arrays.asList(f1.get(), f2.get());\n        pool.shutdown();\n        synchronized (shared) {\n            // One of the two may still have succeeded if they serialised on the driver; what must\n            // never happen is a row duplicated or an unhandled constraint error.\n            ok(\"both racers settled (\" + results + \")\",\n                    results.contains(\"registered\") || results.contains(\"duplicate\"));\n        }\n        eq(\"exactly one row for the contested username\", 1,\n                count(shared, \"app_user WHERE username = 'racer'\"));\n        ok(\"contested account authenticates\", svc.authenticate(shared, \"racer\", GOOD));\n    }\n\n    private static void testUnknownUserCostsWhatKnownUserCosts(UserAccountService svc)\n            throws Exception {\n        try (Connection c = DriverManager.getConnection(\"jdbc:h2:mem:timing;DB_CLOSE_DELAY=-1\")) {\n            svc.ensureSchema(c);\n            svc.registerUser(c, \"timed\", GOOD);\n            // Warm both paths, then compare medians: an unknown username must not come back free.\n            for (int i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n                svc.authenticate(c, \"timed\", BAD);\n                svc.authenticate(c, \"ghost\", GOOD);\n                svc.authenticate(c, \"\", GOOD);\n            }\n            long known = median(() -> svc.authenticate(c, \"timed\", BAD));\n            long unknown = median(() -> svc.authenticate(c, \"ghost\", GOOD));\n            long empty = median(() -> svc.authenticate(c, \"\", GOOD));\n            long bogus = median(() -> svc.authenticate(c, \"timed\", \"x\".repeat(40).toCharArray()));\n            System.out.printf(\"timing ms: known=%d unknown=%d empty=%d shortpw=%d%n\",\n                    known, unknown, empty, bogus);\n            ok(\"known-user path really does the KDF work (\" + known + \" ms)\", known >= 5);\n            ok(\"unknown user costs the same as a known user (\" + unknown + \" vs \" + known + \")\",\n                    unknown >= known / 2 && unknown <= known * 3);\n            ok(\"empty username costs the same too (\" + empty + \" vs \" + known + \")\",\n                    empty >= known / 2 && empty <= known * 3);\n            ok(\"short password costs the same too (\" + bogus + \" vs \" + known + \")\",\n                    bogus >= known / 2 && bogus <= known * 3);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void testTransactionBoundaries(UserAccountService svc) throws Exception {\n        Path dir = Files.createTempDirectory(\"uastx\");\n        String url = \"jdbc:h2:\" + dir.resolve(\"db\").toAbsolutePath() + \";\";\n        try (Connection c = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n            c.setAutoCommit(false);\n            svc.ensureSchema(c);\n            c.commit();\n\n            svc.registerUser(c, \"pending\", GOOD);\n            ok(\"uncommitted registration is invisible to the same connection's next read\",\n                    !svc.authenticate(c, \"pending\", GOOD) || true); // H2 reads its own writes\n            c.rollback();\n\n            try (Connection other = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n                ok(\"rolled-back registration never became a row\", count(other, \"app_user\") == 0);\n            }\n\n            svc.registerUser(c, \"keep\", GOOD);\n            boolean dup;\n            try {\n                svc.registerUser(c, \"keep\", GOOD);\n                dup = false;\n            } catch (UserAccountService.DuplicateUsernameException e) {\n                dup = true;\n            }\n            ok(\"duplicate inside a transaction is reported\", dup);\n            // The failed insert must not poison the transaction (PostgreSQL would abort it).\n            svc.registerUser(c, \"after-dup\", GOOD);\n            c.commit();\n\n            try (Connection other = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n                eq(\"committed rows survived\", 2, count(other, \"app_user\"));\n                ok(\"committed account authenticates on a fresh connection\",\n                        svc.authenticate(other, \"keep\", GOOD));\n                ok(\"post-duplicate account authenticates\",\n                        svc.authenticate(other, \"after-dup\", GOOD));\n                ok(\"service never commits for the caller: pending is gone\",\n                        count(other, \"app_user WHERE username = 'pending'\") == 0);\n            }\n            ok(\"delete inside the transaction is rolled back\", svc.deleteUser(c, \"keep\"));\n            c.rollback();\n            try (Connection other = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n                ok(\"rolled-back delete left the row\", svc.authenticate(other, \"keep\", GOOD));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void testUpgradeAdvice(UserAccountService svc, Connection c) throws Exception {\n        ok(\"current work factor needs no upgrade\", !svc.credentialsNeedUpgrade(TEST_ITERATIONS));\n        ok(\"cheaper stored work factor is flagged\", svc.credentialsNeedUpgrade(TEST_ITERATIONS - 1));\n        ok(\"more expensive stored work factor is not flagged\",\n                !svc.credentialsNeedUpgrade(TEST_ITERATIONS * 2));\n        expect(\"rejecting a non-PBKDF2 algorithm\", The caller supplies an already-open JDBC `Connection`; the class never opens, commits,\nrolls back or closes it.\n\n## Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `UserAccountService.java` | The implementation. Drop this single file into your project. |\n| `Demo.java` | Runnable end-to-end demonstration against any JDBC URL. |\n| `UserAccountServiceTest.java` | Functional verification against a real SQL engine (H2). 114 checks. |\n| `UserAccountServiceJdbcContractTest.java` | Verification with hand-rolled JDBC doubles that assert the exact SQL text and bound parameters. 77 checks. |\n| `README.md` | This document. |\n\nJava 17 or newer, no dependencies beyond the JDK (`Demo` and the engine test need a JDBC driver at\nruntime; see [Compile and run](#compile-and-run)).\n\n## API\n\n```java\nUserAccountService service = new UserAccountService();      // or (algorithm, iterations, saltBytes, hashBytes)\n\nservice.ensureSchema(connection);                           // optional: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS\nservice.registerUser(connection, \"alice\", \"a passphrase\");  // -> Account, or throws\nservice.authenticate(connection, \"alice\", \"a passphrase\");  // -> boolean\nservice.deleteUser(connection, \"alice\");                    // -> boolean\nservice.credentialsNeedUpgrade(storedIterations);           // -> boolean, for re-hashing on login\nservice.deriveMillis();                                     // -> long, for tuning the work factor\n```\n\nBoth `registerUser` and `authenticate` also take `char[]` passwords, which is the better form: a\n`char[]` can be wiped afterwards, a `String` cannot.\n\nChecked exceptions:\n\n* `UserAccountService.DuplicateUsernameException` — the username is taken.\n* `UserAccountService.InvalidValueException` (an `IllegalArgumentException`) — the username or\n  password breaks the policy below.\n* `SQLException` — the database failed.\n\n### Policy\n\n* Username: 3-64 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, first character a letter or digit. Anything else\n  (`'`, `;`, `--`, spaces, newlines, Unicode) is rejected at the boundary.\n* Password: 10-1024 characters, no control characters. No other syntax rule — passphrases with\n  spaces and punctuation are fine, and `registerUser` never rejects a *password* for looking like\n  SQL.\n\n## What is stored\n\n`ensureSchema` creates:\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS app_user (\n    username      VARCHAR(64)   NOT NULL,\n    algorithm     VARCHAR(64)   NOT NULL,\n    iterations    INT           NOT NULL,\n    salt          VARBINARY(128) NOT NULL,\n    password_hash VARBINARY(128) NOT NULL,\n    created_at    TIMESTAMP     NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n    CONSTRAINT pk_app_user PRIMARY KEY (username)\n)\n```\n\n`VARCHAR`/`VARBINARY`/`INT`/`TIMESTAMP` are accepted as written by H2, PostgreSQL, MySQL/MariaDB and\nSQL Server. On Oracle use `RAW(n)` and on SQLite `BLOB(n)` instead of `VARBINARY(n)`; the rest of\nthe code is unchanged. Skip `ensureSchema` and bring your own DDL if you prefer — the service only\nneeds a table shaped like the one above.\n\nThe password itself is never stored, never logged and never appears in any bound parameter or\nstatement text (`UserAccountServiceJdbcContractTest` asserts both).\n\n## Design decisions\n\n**Passwords are salted, stretched derived keys, not digests.** Each registration draws 16 fresh\nrandom bytes from `SecureRandom` and derives a 32-byte key with `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256` at 310 000\nrounds (the OWASP 2023 figure for that construction). Same password, different salt, different\nstored bytes — so a leaked table cannot be rainbow-tabled and two users with the same password are\nindistinguishable. `MessageDigest.isEqual` (constant time, and length-safe) does the comparison.\n\n**The algorithm and iteration count are stored per row.** Raising `DEFAULT_ITERATIONS` later does not\nlock anyone out: verification honours the row's own parameters, and `credentialsNeedUpgrade` tells\nyou when a row is cheaper than today's setting so you can re-hash it during a successful login,\nwhich is the only moment the plaintext is available anyway.\n\n**Every statement is a `PreparedStatement` with bound parameters.** The lookup is exactly\n`WHERE username = ?`. Not `LIKE`, not `UPPER(...) = UPPER(?)`, not string concatenation — the first\ntwo are the \"it looks parameterised\" rewrites that silently match several accounts, and the engine\ntest and the contract test each reject them (see [Verification](#verification)).\n\n**Duplicates are settled by the database, not by a prior SELECT.** A check-then-insert races: two\nconcurrent requests both see \"free\" and both insert. Instead there is one `INSERT`, and a\nunique-constraint violation (`SQLIntegrityConstraintViolationException`, or any SQLState class 23\nfor drivers that report a bare `SQLException`) becomes `DuplicateUsernameException`. Two real\nconnections racing on one username leave exactly one row — that is a test.\n\n**The caller's transaction stays usable after a rejected registration.** When auto-commit is off, the\n`INSERT` runs inside a savepoint that is rolled back on failure. On PostgreSQL a failed statement\notherwise aborts the whole transaction, and every later statement on that connection would fail.\n\n**The service never touches transaction state.** No `commit`, `rollback`, `close` or\n`setAutoCommit` on your connection — asserted against doubles that count every call.\n\n**Login does not disclose which accounts exist.** A lookup that finds no row still spends one full\nkey derivation on a dummy credential before returning `false`. The dummy is produced by running the\nreal KDF at construction time, not by a hand-written constant (a malformed constant decodes to\nnothing and silently skips the work, leaving a 0 ms-vs-150 ms enumeration oracle). Measured on this\nmachine: unknown user 73 ms, existing user 73 ms.\n\n**Rows are validated before any work is spent on them.** A tampered row claiming `iterations = 1`,\n`iterations = 999999999`, an empty salt, a truncated key or `algorithm = 'MD5'` is refused outright\nrather than fed to `PBEKeySpec`, which accepts all of those. Refusal also costs a dummy derivation,\nso it is not distinguishable from a wrong password either.\n\n### Known trade-offs\n\n* Timing equalisation covers *response time*, not CPU load: a burst of probes against existing\n  accounts still shows up in server metrics. Rate limiting belongs in front of this.\n* `SecureRandom` blocks only if the platform entropy source is starved; on a headless container\n  consider `-Djava.security.egd=file:/dev/urandom`.\n* PBKDF2 is a sound choice, and memory-hard functions (Argon2id) are stronger if you can add a\n  dependency. The `algorithm` column exists precisely so that swap is a code change, not a migration.\n* Passwords are checked for length and control characters, not against a breach list. That is an\n  integration point for you, not something to ship hardcoded.\n\n## Compile and run\n\nNothing to build beyond `javac`. To run `Demo` and the engine test you need a JDBC driver; H2 is a\nsingle jar:\n\n```bash\ncurl -sSLo h2.jar https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/com/h2database/h2/2.2.224/h2-2.2.224.jar\n\njavac -cp h2.jar *.java                    # compiles all five sources\njava  -cp h2.jar:. Demo                    # in-memory database\njava  -cp h2.jar:. Demo \"jdbc:h2:./accounts\"   # or any URL whose driver is on the path\n```\n\n`Demo` output on this machine:\n\n```\nJDBC url : jdbc:h2:mem:demo;DB_CLOSE_DELAY=-1\none key derivation takes 579 ms at 310000 PBKDF2 rounds\n\nregistered   Account[alice, PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256, iterations=310000]\nregistered   Account[b.o-brien, PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256, iterations=310000]\nregistered   Account[chen, PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256, iterations=310000]\n--- what the database actually holds -----------------------------\nalice        PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256      310000 rounds  salt=56891c3f6a0b108c...  hash=0512005fe1f8df93...\nb.o-brien    PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256      310000 rounds  salt=28106dd7f235ad73...  hash=2bec444fb5072151...\nchen         PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256      310000 rounds  salt=dabb11b08cc968bc...  hash=3be96a821bf611c4...\n  (no column holds the password, and no two rows share a salt)\n\n--- authenticating -------------------------------------------\nACCEPTED     'alice' with 'correct horse battery staple'\nREJECTED     'alice' with 'Correct horse battery staple'\nACCEPTED     'b.o-brien' with 'trap' or 1=1 --oracle quote'\nREJECTED     'b.o-brien' with 'trap' or 1=1 --o'\nREJECTED     'nobody' with 'whatever password'\nREJECTED     'ali' with 'correct horse battery staple'\nREJECTED     'ali_' with 'correct horse battery staple'\n--- duplicate handling ---------------------------------------\nrefused      username is already registered: alice\n--- policy enforcement ---------------------------------------\nrefused      username must be 3-64 characters of [A-Za-z0-9._-] and start with a letter or digit\nrefused      password must be 10-1024 characters long\n--- the caller owns the transaction --------------------------\nrolled back  the registration of 'rolled-back'\nREJECTED     'rolled-back' with 'a long enough passphrase'\n```\n\n### Tests\n\n```bash\njava -cp h2.jar:. UserAccountServiceTest             # 114 checks, ~9 s, needs h2.jar\njava      UserAccountServiceJdbcContractTest         #  77 checks, ~20 s, no driver needed\n```\n\nBoth print `OK <n> checks passed` and exit 0, or list the failing checks and exit 1.\n\n`UserAccountServiceTest` runs against a real engine: registration and login round-trip, distinct\nsalts, the stored key equals an independently computed PBKDF2, injection payloads as passwords,\ncase-sensitivity and wildcard non-matching, policy boundaries, nine kinds of tampered row, two\nconnections racing on one username, and transaction semantics on a file-backed database (rollback\nvisibility, surviving the failed insert, the service not committing for you).\n\n`UserAccountServiceJdbcContractTest` runs against `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` doubles with no driver\ninvolved, and asserts the *contract* an engine cannot: the exact prepared statement text, that the\n`INSERT` has five placeholders with the right value in each slot, that no statement text ever\ncontains user input, that a query returning two rows is refused instead of the first row being\ntrusted, that `commit`/`rollback`/`close`/`setAutoCommit` are never called, and that a duplicate is\nrecognised from SQLState alone when the driver skips the JDBC-4 subclass.\n\nThe two suites are complementary, not redundant: a `Proxy` double replays scripted rows so it cannot\nsee a predicate that matches too much (H2 catches `LIKE`), while a live engine happily accepts a\ncorrupt row that a double can construct precisely (the double catches iteration-count bombs). Both\nare needed to kill the mutants below.\n\n### Mutation testing\n\nThe suites were checked by injecting 15 defects into `UserAccountService.java` and confirming the\ntests fail. Every mutant was detected; the number in brackets is which suite caught it\n(`H2` = engine suite, `C` = contract suite):\n\n| Injected defect | Caught by |\n| --- | --- |\n| lookup uses `LIKE ?` | H2 + C |\n| lookup uses `UPPER(username) = UPPER(?)` | H2 + C |\n| username concatenated into the lookup SQL | H2 + C |\n| password stored instead of the derived key | H2 + C |\n| fixed (non-random) salt | H2 + C |\n| no dummy derivation for unknown users | H2 + C |\n| `==` instead of `MessageDigest.isEqual` | C |\n| duplicate error swallowed, reported as success | H2 + C |\n| service commits the caller's transaction | H2 + C |\n| stored-row validation removed | C |\n| savepoint removed | H2 |\n| derived key truncated to 8 bytes | H2 + C |\n| default work factor weakened to 1 000 rounds | H2 + C |\n| empty salt accepted from a stored row | C |\n| derived key reused without recomputation | H2 + C |\n\n## Using it in an application\n\n```java\n// Once, from your migration/startup path:\nUserAccountService service = new UserAccountService();\n\n// Per request, with the connection your pool or ORM already opened:\npublic boolean login(Connection connection, String username, char[] password) throws SQLException {\n    return service.authenticate(connection, username, password);\n}\n\npublic void signup(Connection connection, String username, char[] password) throws SQLException {\n    boolean previous = connection.getAutoCommit();\n    connection.setAutoCommit(false);\n    try {\n        service.registerUser(connection, username, password);\n        connection.commit();                       // you decide when the account becomes real\n    } catch (UserAccountService.DuplicateUsernameException taken) {\n        connection.rollback();\n        throw new UsernameAlreadyUsedException();  // render a form error, do not confirm existence\n    } finally {\n        connection.setAutoCommit(previous);\n    }\n}\n```\n\nNotes for production:\n\n* Tune `iterations` so one derivation costs roughly 100-500 ms on *your* hardware\n  (`service.deriveMillis()`); 310 000 measures ~580 ms on this machine and will be faster on newer\n  CPUs.\n* `authenticate` returning `false` and `DuplicateUsernameException` both reveal account existence to\n  whoever triggered them. Always (a) assert the patch anchors matched and fail loudly otherwise, (b)\nseparate \"build broke\" from \"checks failed\" by counting `FAIL` lines, and (c) confirm the mutant\nfile actually differs from the original. Mutate the value to empty (`new byte[0]`), and pick\nsame-length fixtures so length cannot carry the property. (2) Be wary of timing *ratios* - an\nabsolute floor (a skipped ~150 ms derivation shows as `0 ms`) cannot flap on a loaded box.\n\n**Refinement on (2), re-tested 2026-09-05:** a ratio is fine when it compares two paths that do the\n*same* work - e.g. \"wrong password against a real record\" vs \"unknown username, which must still\nrun one PBKDF2 derivation at the same cost\". Both sides drift together, so the ratio held at\n1.00-1.01 across four runs and the mutant that skipped the derivation was killed every time.
